# Code map: default.xbe

An index of the default.xbe functions and addresses the project relies on: what each one does,
how sure we are, and where it's used or written up. Look here before tracing a function; it may
be done already. Addresses are virtual addresses in the US `default.xbe` (base 0x10000; `.text`
0x12000-0x3118b0, `.rdata` from 0x386340, `.data` from 0x3b5b00).

* `FUN_0022c4d0` is an address where Ghidra made a function: its C is in
  `decompiled/xbe/ghidra/c/` (`python tools/fn.py 22c4d0` prints it). `0x22d0b0` is code Ghidra
  didn't split out: read it in `decompiled/xbe/default.text.asm`. Data (tables, vtables,
  globals) is written `0x3bd9d4`; `tools/xbe_peek.py f|i|x ADDR` reads values. Search the page
  for an address's hex digits without `0x` or leading zeros (`22c4d0`, `7c720`) to find it in
  either form.
* `bit N` is a bit index (bit 0 is mask 1, bit 3 is mask 8); `flag 0x40` is a mask.
* A row covers a function; the places inside it that a page cites, and the constants it reads,
  are in that row. The constants also have their own table at the end.
* Name tables are `{length, name, value}` records. Their address here is where the game's text
  parser starts reading: the first name pointer, 4 bytes past the first record.
* Offsets: `char+0x490` is a character object's field, `type+0x220` its character type's
  (`[char+0xc]`), `skill+0x1c` its skill object's (`[char+0x5a4]`), `weapon+0x218` a weapon
  object's, `data+0xd0` the weapon data a weapon points at from +0x228.

**How sure** (the third column):
* **code**: read from the disassembly or Ghidra's C (static, not run).
* **live**: also checked in the running game (xemu's gdb stub, or a take that matches it).
* **data**: read from the game's data files (`decompiled/xml/`), with the code that uses it.
* **inferred**: deduced from its use, the data or the footage, not read line by line.
* **guess**: a reading nobody has checked.

Brackets narrow a word: the part that is only inferred, or the source's own rating (medium,
low).

**Where** (the fourth column): a docs page and its section, a source file under `viewer/src/`
(`play_shots.rs` for `src/bin/play_shots.rs`), a ticket. `todo/35` leads, spec or notes are the
research in the git-ignored `todo/35-*/` folder; `todo/damage-factor` is the lead's code
question in `todo/damage-factor/answer.md`. "This page" means it was read for this index.

**Adding a row.** Put it in its area's table, at its address. One line: what it does, with the
game's own names (enum names, strings, attribute names); how sure; where it's used. When a row
is wrong, correct it and say so in your report. When two places disagree, read the code, record
what it does in the row and say which place was wrong.

For a living character, yes if all hold: +0x13c returns 0 or the object is a grenade (24); the object's `+0x26` bit 0x20 is set; the character isn't dead (`+0x70` bit 0) or the object's owner (`+0x3c`); their scene roots differ. Characters need a clear ray from the centre and their entry is at their position; a blast with flag 0x10 (the Gas's) skips characters whose +0x70 bit 0 is set. Other game objects (scenery) are taken with no ray and their entry stays at the blast's centre (normal from the object toward it), so their distance in FUN_0022c4d0 is 0. The static world's entry is at the blast's centre with its normal straight up: that entry lays a blast's scorch (FUN_0022ca20). Loose physics objects are pushed by (1.2 - d / r)^2 x damage x 1.5 (0x3bc6f0), only when the type's +0x234 is 1 or more. **Correction** (#72; todo/weapons-plan answers-batch1 §9): there is no radius test. It walks the overlap list of the blast's own physics ball (FUN_0022c2e0; the list at [[blast+0x34]+0x18]+0x30, count at +4, 8-byte entries at +0xc, FUN_000e0af0 walking it from the end) by each body's client-data kind (body+0x9c, the PID_ names): 0 an object's body: projectiles skipped, the rest added at the centre with no ray and pushed when h_e39b994b (type+0x234) is 1 or more (not a gas blast, not object types 2 / 0x1a / 0x1f); 1 a character's collision-bone body: the ray from the centre to the bone body's position (FUN_002b3970) against the list only, the character itself excluded, and if clear one entry per character at its position (vtable +0x40), normal from it to the centre; never pushed; other kinds (2 the terrain, 10 the character's phantom, 3 other blasts) the entry keyed 0 at the centre, normal up: the scorch's. The demo meets the ball with the hit parts' surfaces plus a fitted margin (`BLAST_PART_REACH`, 0.62 m: the engine's overlap rule isn't in the game's code). **#280:** a dead character (char+0x7d8 bit 1 clear, i.e. its ragdoll built) met through a bone body is pushed after all: its root body char+0x500 (0x224f89-0x224fa5) takes the same 1.5 x (1.2 - d / r)^2 x damage impulse at the blast's position, away from it, when h_e39b994b is 1 or more (todo/280 answer §2.5). | code | scenery.md (debris push, line of sight; Missiles); `play_scenery.rs`; #73 spec §5; todo/damage-factor; this page; `play_missile.rs` (a body behind cover is skipped; the scenery's share flat, `ObjectBlast::flat`; the scorch; `deal_blasts`, `BLAST_PART_REACH`, `BLAST_BODY_REACH`); #72 spec §5, §10; `play_ragdoll.rs` (`Ragdoll::blast`, `blast_corpse`, #259) |
| `FUN_00225000` | Resets a blast pool object for its next use (through FUN_002250b0): the sweep counter byte +3 = 3 (FUN_00225440), flags 0x40 and 0x80 cleared (0x225058). A bolt's launch (FUN_0022bac0) takes its object this way and never writes +3. So no bolt has a sweep phase of its own (#64): the MNR-7's three darts, launched in one frame, sweep on the same ticks; take18's impacts 0.1 s apart are darts whose wall lies past the first sweep's 8.0 m. | code, live (#287 take18, take19) | todo/weapons-plan/answers-batch1.md §1; `play_bolts.rs` (`SWEEP_TICKS`); play.md § The Rail CLVR, the Ion Beamer and the MNR-7 Bio |
| `FUN_00225060` | A blast's damage now: +0x20. With flag 0x40 and a life (+0x30) above 0 it would be FUN_00223780(+0x20, 1 - +0x28 / +0x30), a taper over its life, its curve mode from the weapon type's +0x20c (h_fb124e6c). Nothing sets flag 0x40: the pool's init (FUN_00225000) clears 0x40 and 0x80 (0x225058), FUN_0022bfc0 sets only 0x80 from the hit, the copy at 0x226010 copies its source, and no write in 0x223000-0x234000 sets it. So the taper is unreachable. | code | grenades.md § Sentry |
| `FUN_00225220` | Tick of the blast pool (from FUN_000d9a20): counts each object's delay (+0x2c) to 0, then its life (+0x28). A weapon blast (flag bit 0) gathers through FUN_00225c40; a bullet flies (FUN_00225440, or FUN_00225c90 with a physics body); its effect follows (FUN_0021c380, life / start); then FUN_00224770 deals. A bullet whose life (+0x28) is out ends unmoved (FUN_00225440's first test); an ended one (flag 4) is released the next tick (0x2253e2-0x22541d). | code | grenades.md § Sentry; `play_bolts.rs` (`Bolt::advance`; the life / start parameter isn't kept: no bolt's flight effect varies with it) |
| `FUN_00225440` | Flies a bullet-type 0 projectile (FUN_0022bac0) in the blast pool: moves it by its speed (+0x1c) x dt along +0x10 and every third tick sweeps a ray from its last checked point, adding what it meets (FUN_00224320). grenades.md had it move a grenade's blast (fixed): weapon blasts don't move. The sweep counter is byte +3 (3 from FUN_00225000's reset), counted down each tick; at 0 the ray (FUN_000ddd10) runs from the last checked point (+0x70) over the way moved plus this tick's step, then +3 = 3: ticks 3, 6, 9 ... from the launch, 0.1 s apart. No hit: +0x70 = the position. A hit: the bolt set 0.1 % short of it, the body gathered, the bolt ended (flag 4) unless it passes or ricochets; FUN_00224770 deals it in the same tick (damage, hole, effect, sound) and the next tick releases the bolt. Between sweeps nothing is tested, so a bolt flies on into a wall for up to two ticks. The pool ticks after the objects' update in FUN_000d9a20 (0xd9ae2, then 0xd9b07), so the launch frame would be tick 1; the takes count from the frame after (answers-batch1.md §1). Ricochet spends n1 on a kind-2 non-character hit, else n2 (n2 also when n1 is out); never with the pass flag. #238, the rest of the rule: with flag 0x20 a hit on a game object that isn't a character (the record's +4 set, its type's +4 not 3) while byte +1 (n1) is left takes one off it, a character's while byte +2 (n2) is left one off that, the object's handle (its +0x6c, or that one's +8) goes to +0x40 for the next sweep's filter to leave out, and the bolt flies on from the hit point; anything else ends it (the terrain has no object). Without the flag, while n1 or n2 is left: a kind-2 non-character hit takes n1 (n2 when n1 is 0, 0x225b0a), any other n2, and the way (+0x10) is turned about the result's normal (2 (d.n) n taken off); its count at 0 ends it. #136: the demo flies each bolt so (its sweeps against the world as it is then, less the blockers, the characters where they are, the Sentries; the hit dealt in that frame), where before it picked the hit at the shot. #56: a pass-through deals each thing crossed the full launch roll (no attenuation on the path): #287 take22, the Ion Beamer through a spike (-14.44) into a Shrike (-20.21 = x 1.4) on one shot, the Shrike's 0.09 s after the spike's (the sweeps at ticks 3 and 6). | code (the launch frame's count: the takes say the frame after), live (#287 take22) | grenades.md § Sentry; todo/weapons-plan/answers-batch1.md §1, §6; play.md § Bolts and their hits; `play_bolts.rs` (`Bolt::advance`, `Bolt::after_hit`, `fly_bolts`, `sweep`, HIT_BACK) |

| `FUN_0022bfc0` | Makes a weapon blast (pool at world+0x1fc, flag bit 0) where a hit happened: damage +0x20 = a new roll (FUN_0022c940) after a direct hit, the source blast's (FUN_00225060) after a blast, x the factor; radius +0x24 = Damage radius; life +0x28 / +0x30 = h_04ea9251; flag 0x10 = h_0e373132; flag 0x80 = the hit's +0x58 bit 1, the other bits kept (0x22c0b7-0x22c0ca; nothing sets 0x40); delay +0x2c = 0.05 + 0.2 x U. Then x the hit's factor (+0x4c), the push (type+0x234; 2 for a blast hit when 0), and x 0.7 with EVT_SHOT_BLOCKED_BY_FRIEND (34) when FUN_0004b9c0 holds. grenades.md said a blast isn't tapered by distance and this adds "a second taper for some targets"; the distance rule is this one (fixed). **#280:** the record's +0x54 is the push itself (type+0x234, h_e39b994b; 2.0 for a blast hit when 0) and its byte +0x5c bit 3 (0x22c766) is the inverse of the hit source's +0x16 bit 3 (no writer of that source bit found, so the hit bit reads as set): the gate of the CBT_DEAD ragdoll shove in FUN_00115f90 (todo/280 answer §2.2). | code, live (the half share fits #73's HP logs) | #73 spec §6; todo/damage-factor; play.md § Missiles, § Damage; `play_missile.rs`, `play_scenery.rs`, `bf/weapon.rs`; grenades.md § Sentry; `play_damage.rs` (`Hit`); **(#79 Round 2 Q4)** the thrower's share is the blast's own: a core blast (`h_011cb154` true) within r' / 2 gives the whole roll, x 0.7 (own squad) x the victim's step (0.6 on STANDARD) = 0.42 of a roll (Tex -50.6 of a Roller, Brutus -13.9 of a Thermo); the Frag's half share 0.21 (#112's, the demo keeps 0.2: grenades.md § Damage) |

| `FUN_00226cc0` | Projectile tick (vtable +0x6c): steering; an aim point within 1 m or over 60 degrees off (dot < 0.5, 0x3a4f74) is given up for a point its velocity x 200 ahead (vtable +0x54 x 200 at 0x227038: 6 km at 30 m/s; this row said 200 m); the proximity fuse every 0.25 s within Damage radius x 0.375, for a human player's missile (FUN_00112800); life (+0x1d8), then the second timer (+0x1d4); at life 0 MIRV modes 1 and 2 split (FUN_00227f80), mode 1 also at its apogee. The fuse needs a target or a side other than 2 (0x22731c-0x22732d), so a child without a target has none. The 60-degree test is against the node's -z (FUN_0001fdd0, 0x226fdc). The aim point (+0x1f0) is refreshed each tick from the target's (+0x1ec) vtable +0x40 position (0x2270fe), its node: a character's hips. The give-up (within 1 m of the stored aim point, or > 60 degrees off the node's -z) comes before the fuse; it drops the target (FUN_00226360 with EAX = 0, 0x2270d4). The fuse runs after the timers every 0.25 s (+0x218), to the target's node. **The fuse's period** (#72): its clock +0x218 gains the tick's dt while it's under 0.25 and on the tick it's 0.25 or more is cleared and the check runs (the fuse block's `if (t < 0.25) t += dt; else { t = 0; check }`): every 9th tick at 30 Hz, 0.3 s. The check also needs the owner (+0x1b4) to be a character (object type 3). With flag 4 alone (the Sweeper V: no flag 8 / 0x40) the steering's direction is the missile's own velocity every tick: a straight line at the bullet speed. **#239:** without flag 4, and without flag 8 / 0x40 or still in the straight phase (+0x230 > 0), the steering is skipped (0x226dd9-0x226dfc): the body keeps its velocity under the world's gravity (FUN_0016a250) and its template's damping (FUN_00158f80), the iKhan-GPL's lobbed round. The apogee split is at 0x2272b4-0x2272ea: mode 1, the body's vertical speed (vtable +0x54) under 0.5 ([0x3a4f74]) and +0x230 at 0. the root (char+0x500 = the body of the character's node char+0x6c, or a body whose node's parent is that node) gets inertia factors 0.5 and mass 9 (0x40cbe8), so I = 4.5 kg m^2 isotropic; the others factors 0.25 and mass 4.5, I = 1.125; the velocity v (char+0x470 through vtable +0x54, or char+0x6ec in modes 2 / 3) is applied as an impulse 9 v on every body at its centre (FUN_002b3bc0: v on the root, 2 v on a 4.5 kg body); the world's gravity controller (world+0xa44's +0x1c) is added to each core; With the skill on and its vtable +0x7c true (Tex's, Flint's), three buttons send CT_TOGGLE_SPECIAL instead of what they do: black (VB 63, the medkit shortcut) at 0x134367; Y (VB 10) at 0x134db4-0x134dde, in place of CT 9 (the weapon switch); X let go short of its hold time, or with nothing to hold it for (0x134c2b-0x134c4a: on the release, the held time +0x4c above 0 and under the hold time +0x50; then the reload path at 0x134c50; the hold time's handling read, not run; the demo's R ends it on the press instead, its choice), when a reload could start (FUN_00120c50), also setting +0xa1 bit 2, a reload asked for. Otherwise (no such skill on, or no reload possible) that X release calls the reload itself, FUN_00120d40 at 0x134c9d: a tap of X reloads (play.md § Ammo; `play_ammo.rs`: R). **Correction:** this row had LT (VB 7) on the black button's path; LT's value is read just before it (0x134316) but goes its own way (#35 take26 20.5 s: LT doesn't end Tex's berserk). Each frame (0x134a73-0x134ab7) it calls the reload, FUN_00120d40, while the gun in hand's clip is 0 or that bit is set, clearing the bit when the reload starts: an empty gun reloads by itself, and the reload X asked for follows berserk's end. The left stick past 0.9 (0x3a50b8) sends CT_DASH (19) at 0x134f52. Black up: the saved pair back (FUN_0014d730) unless none, bit 0 cleared (0x1345c6, every frame the section runs with Black up). This state (+0x68, +0x6c, +0xa1, +0xa0 bit 7) is the controller's, the player's: a hand-over (FUN_000c8fa0) leaves it, so a saved pair comes back on the new member. **LT** (VB 7, 0x13460e-0x134a30): nothing selected (0x134612, before the lock's test): "Inventory is empty" (h_1393ebea) each frame LT is held while +0x68 is 0 or -1 (0x1349c3: so 3 s from the release), +0x68 = -1 held or not (0x13451b, 0x1349df); LT reading 0 clears +0xa0 bit 7 (0x13467e); +0x68 another stack (or -1): the charge zeroed, +0x68 = 0 once LT is up (0x1346a7), nothing used; FUN_000abbb0 refusing: its message for 3 s at the press (unless bit 7), +0x68 = -1 (even with LT up, 0x13497f: so the next frame clears it again, and a press landing on such a frame shows nothing; inferred as the cause of #196's two silent presses at full health, take13 / take15); bit 7 set (a use made with LT still down): +0x68 = -1 (0x1346f6, 0x134957); else LT down: +0x68 = the stack (0x134751), a non-grenade used at once (charge 0) and bit 7 set (0x1348af), a grenade charged at 1.6 a second (0x3a5088) to FUN_000abf30's cap, the meter; LT up with a charge: thrown at clamp((charge - 0.3) x 1 / 0.7, 0.01, 1) (0x3a50b0, 0x3a54c8). ## Faces

The face controller (char+0x5b8), the per-type face table, the sound library's viseme tracks, the
blink and the expressions (#255, `todo/255-facial-animation/leads.md`).

| `FUN_00143380` | The face controller's constructor (0x64 bytes; ESI the object, the argument the type's face table [type+0x1ac], char+0x6c the model): the table's face bones (+0x24, 8-byte {bone hash, kind}) found as model nodes (FUN_00235d50), each given node+0x44 bit 5 (so FUN_00234190 never binds a body clip to them) and listed at +0x10; +8 the skeleton the blink plays on (the first face bone's node +0x10 -> +0x64, -1 with no bones); +0x4c = 10.0 (the blink is due at once); +0x58 = +0x5c = 1 (EFE_BASE); the output pose (+0x24) and the 22 weights (+0x34) sized, the base pose applied (FUN_00143820). Made at a character's creation (0x112ae0, the character vtable's +0x9c, at 0x112b37-0x112b87) and spawn setup (FUN_0012f440, 0x12f848-0x12f892); none for a type without `<face>`. Flagging each face bone's node (+0x44 bit 5) is what keeps `Sc_blink` off a lid that is also in `Sc_base` (FUN_00234190 skips flagged nodes): Hawk's four lids are (22 bones in her `Sc_base`), so her blink binds nothing; the lids of every other model with a face table are outside the list (live: take03, Hawk's eye stays open at her resets). ## Effects / ALE

Materials and shaders are here too.

| Address | What it does | How sure | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| `FUN_0001d0c0` | Builds a frame from a direction (y up for a level one): the explosion turned along a missile's way (FUN_0022cbe0), a decal's frame (FUN_0022ca20). The frame's z column is -d (so -z along d), x = up x z, y = z x x; the up hint is world y, or world z when abs(d.y) > 0.9848. The MIRV split builds its frame with it from the parent's velocity. A hit effect's alignment 2 (FUN_0021a850) builds it from the stored surface normal; FUN_0022cbe0 from the hit's velocity. | code | play.md § Missiles, § Bolts and their hits, § Shots (Tracers and beams); todo/73 answer-round4 §Q2; todo/weapons-plan/answers-batch1.md §2; `play_missile.rs` (`split`); `play.rs` (`hit_frame`, `beam_line`: Bevy's `looking_to`) |
| `FUN_00062280` | Binds a texture to a stage (the stage in ECX, the texture in EAX). Given none, it binds the default `[0x4698c4]` = 0x4432e4, a 1 x 1 texture whose texel is FF FF FF FF (opaque white; made at 0x238bb0-0x238c95); `[0x4698c8]` = 0x4432d0 is a 1 x 1 transparent black one. So a material slot left empty samples white: the e40 ocean's empty `texture-0`. | code | todo/146-liquids answer-spec Q2; `level_scene.rs` (`LiquidSurface::layer0`) |
| `0x64d60` | The game's own cube emitter's update (class e0ca2222, no Ghidra function): +0xf4 / +0xf8 / +0xfc = the node's +0x50 / +0x54 / +0x58 curves (Width h_fd22a11c, Height h_efb0f8cf, Depth h_14b74eb2 by its getter 0x64a84-0x64b13), +0xec = MinSpread (+0x5c) in radians, +0xf0 = MaxSpread (+0x60) - MinSpread; then FUN_00065800. Each read at the emitter's time plus this update's step (0x64d60-0x64d89: the time after FUN_00065800's step, #287). | code | `ale_fx.rs` (`CUBE_REACH`, #258; `GAME_COUNT_BEAMS`, #287) |
| `0x64e30` | Spawn of the cube emitter class e0ca2222 (no Ghidra function): a random point in the box, x = Width x r, y = Height x r, z = Depth x r with r in [-1, 1) ((bits >> 9) \| 0x40000000 - 3.0, 0x64f1a-0x6500f): the sizes are half-extents; with node +0x4c bit 0 (h_e2999ffd, `CubeEmitter_OffsetHeight` in the parameter record at 0x40e394, a bool) y = Height x (r + 1), from the emitter up (0x64ee2). Direction: azimuth r x 2 pi and polar MinSpread + r x (MaxSpread - MinSpread) with r in [0, 1) (0x64e38-0x64e9a). Getter / setter 0x64c30 / 0x64c60. The demo reads h_e2999ffd as its own ATTACHED (`ale_fx.rs`), which this contradicts, but under the placed rules (#258) and in the instant rays' beams (#67: the Rails' rings, rgun_perp_s.emt, a box 3.2 m high growing 116 m a second with OffsetHeight set, strewn along the ray ahead of the muzzle). Its three arguments (`ret 0xc`): the particle's age, its place and an added velocity, both in the emitter's frame from FUN_00065800's loop; the place is that + the box's point + the direction x pressure (+0xac) x the age, the velocity the direction x pressure + the added one (0x65001-0x65075), both then put through the emitter's frame (+0xb8, 0x66160, 0x621c0), and the fields asked (+0xb4) before the add (+0xb8) (#287). | code | todo/73 answer-round4 §Q5; `ale_fx.rs` (`CUBE_REACH`, `ATTACHED`, `Compiled::game_spawn`, `ALE_DUMP`: slava-fuzz's births match it, #290) |
| `0x65320` | ALE emitter constructor: sets the curve base values (100 a second, 2 s), the editor's defaults, unused once a curve has keys. | code | this page |
| `FUN_00065640` | ALE emitter instance constructor (vtable 0x3a3318, whose +0x10 is FUN_00065800 and +0x8c the LOD setter 0x66a20): its time +0x80 and last batch's time +0xa8 = 0, LOD +0x8c = 1.0, and the first-update flag +0xb4 = 1, the only place it's set: InitialParticles come once. | code | play.md § Missiles; `ale_fx.rs` (`game_count`) |
| `FUN_00065800` | ALE emitter update. Count = trunc(elapsed x Emitter_Frequency), + InitialParticles on the first update, + the EmitCount keys in [t_last, t], then trunc(Emitter_LODCurve(LOD +0x8c) x count) (0x659ed-0x65a12). t_last is held while the count is 0 and the frequency isn't; on emitting, t_last = t and the fraction is lost. Runs once a game frame: cut each update, its count depends on the step. Node fields: +0x30 Frequency, +0x34 InitLifeSpan, +0x38 Pressure, +0x3c EmitCount, +0x40 VelocityApproach, +0x44 InitialParticles, +0x48 LODCurve. **Addition (#287):** the batch is spread over the emitter's move since its last batch, in place and age: the move over the count (0x65a73-0x65aa7), each particle stepped back by it and its age by elapsed / count, newest at the emitter (0x65ca2-0x65ce5, the spawn through vtable +0xc0); with the particle system's frame the emitter's own (its +0x2c against the emitter's +8, 0x65c3a-0x65c8b) the place steps are zeroed: all at the emitter. With time elapsed, the move over the elapsed time x VelocityApproach (+0x40) goes to the spawn too, as a velocity (0x65b49-0x65c16; its sign not checked). **Read in full (#287):** the step (+0xbc, 0x2a5b10) first, then Pressure (+0x38 -> +0xac), InitLifeSpan (+0x34 -> +0xb0) and Frequency (+0x30) at the stepped time (0x6584b-0x658ae); on the first update the last place (+0x9c..+0xa4) is set to the emitter's own local translation as it stands then (+0x6c..+0x74, the translation of its +0x48 matrix, relative to its parent: 0x61150 multiplies it by the parent's into the world frame +0xb8; where +0x48 is written, before or after this, isn't traced, so whether a moving emitter's first batch spreads back to its start is open). The loop (0x65ca2-0x65ce5, through the spawn +0xc0): particle k of n is born elapsed x (1 - k / n) old (0x65a87-0x65a99 the 1 / n, 0x65c1a-0x65c33 elapsed / n) and at (1 - k / n) of the way back to the last place in the emitter's frame (0xa0c60 maps it there), the newest at the emitter with age 0; the frame test (0x65c3a-0x65c8b) zeroes the place steps only, not the ages. Then the last place is the current one (0x65b0a-0x65b18) and the last time += elapsed (0x65cfd-0x65d0c). **(#308)** It births into another node, the one at +0x94 (asked through its vtable +0x1c whether it's finished, with +0xb0 / +0xbc around the births and its own +0xc0 for each), and finishes itself (vtable +0xa4) when that node is: an effect stopped by taking its emitters out (0x2abcd0, mask 1) leaves that node and the particles it holds. | code | todo/73 answer-round3 §3.2; play.md § Missiles, § Shots (Tracers and beams); `ale_fx.rs` (`game_count`, `EMIT_LOD`, `GAME_FRAME`, `GAME_COUNT_BEAMS`, `Batch::ages`) |
| `FUN_000661e0` / `0x664c0` / `0x66650` | The ALE kill field (class h_027b6cd9): its constructor (FUN_000661e0) keeps its two parameters h_09df410d and h_1bf425dd (KillField_Width / Height, 10 m in every node) at +0x30 / +0x34, which its test doesn't read; its update (0x664c0, no Ghidra function) takes its plane from its place each step, the point its translation and the normal its frame's +y; its test (0x66650, no Ghidra function) kills a particle on the plane's far side (behind the normal), and one that crosses it within the step, where it crosses. The instant rays' flight effects carry one placed by the user parameter (tracer.fld, rgun_s_kill.fld: 200 m x it out along the ray). | code | play.md § Shots (Tracers and beams); `ale_fx.rs` (`CLASS_KILL_FIELD`, `Compiled::beam`, #237) |
| `0x60f90` | An ALE node instance's spawn check (its vtable +0xb4 in the appearances' vtables 0x390a68 / 0x390b60; no Ghidra function), which the emitter's spawn calls before the add (0x64e30, at 0x6510d): with its +4 bit 0x40000000 (set by +0xb0, 0x60f30, when a child has flag 0x10), each such child, a field, tests the new particle through its +0xac (the kill field's 0x66650: behind its plane, or crossing it within the step, it's taken); one that takes it fails the spawn (0x80004005) and the particle isn't made. Otherwise the add, +0xb8 (0x2a14b0): a particle from the free list at 0x46b030, the pool grown (0x2a28c0) when it's empty, so an appearance's count has no cap. | code | play.md § Shots (Tracers and beams, #287: neither thins the Rails' near rings) |
| `0x66a00` | An ALE node instance's setters (its vtable, 0x3901a0 for the sphere emitter): 0x66a00 the user parameter +0x88 (the sparam its curves are read at, FUN_00065800 0x658a3; the vtable's +0x60), 0x66a20 the LOD +0x8c (+0x8c), 0x66a40 the time +0x80; 0x66a60 / 0x66a80 / 0x66aa0 get the sparam, the time, the lifespan +0x84. A node instance starts with +0x88 = 0 (0x65663); its effect hands it the parameter through 0x2abaa0 and the LOD through 0x2abad0. | code | `ale_fx.rs` (`PLACED_VARIANTS`, #258) |

FUN_00065800 uses it (0x65a82) for the emitter's last place in the emitter's own frame, the batch's move. | code | `ale_fx.rs` (`Batch::ages`, #287) |

On all 141 terrain materials in the data the detail is the colour texture; 140 name a P8 normal map (mp7's h_1c7f8bd5 none). The class's name is `BF_DetailTextureBlend` (its hash h_0ec4edf0; the game material library's name table 0x3f7ce0, its description "Terrain Detail-Bump-Alpha Blend" at 0x3f7c98), its interface 23.23_IBF_TerrainMaterial (0x3a1c04). | `FUN_000dcc10` | Uploads the world shaders' two lights (4 registers from c16: c16 slot 0's direction (w 0), c17 its colour (w 1), c18 slot 1's direction (w 0.5), c19 its colour (w 2)). **Corrected (#143):** its first argument picks the *indoor* pair (1: the table 0x41b428, the lights marked h_e02aba1c, the light's +0x94 bit 1) or the *outdoor* pair (0: 0x41b468, h_1f416687, bit 2), not a terrain and an objects pair: the wrapper material's draw 0x9f3c0 passes 1 for LU_INDOOR and 0 for LU_OUTDOOR (the object's `light-usage`, its +0x44, LU_ names at 0x3bf954), and each view's draw starts with the outdoor pair (FUN_000dcc10(0, vision) at 0xda2aa). A light with both flags is in both pairs. The pairs are filled at a level's load (world+0xc50 bit 3) from the world's lights (+0x22c) whose +0x94 has bits 6 and 4 set and bit 3 clear, in the list's order: a slot rule on the light type's +0x50 bit 3 never applies (that byte is the parsed bool h_ee2faf73, 0x192cb5), so the first light it meets lands in slot 1 and the second moves it to slot 0; no level has more than two lights in a pair. **Corrected (#4):** the slot rule does apply: the light *type*'s +0x50 is a bitfield the light type copy builds (0x14e320-0x14e3f4: bit 0 from the parsed +0x14, bit 1 +0x2c, bit 2 enable-bump +0x6c, bit 3 enable-specular +0x6d, bit 4 h_ee2faf73 +0x50), and a light with bit 3 (enable-specular: common's key h_e1cac6f7 and h_157b3160) takes slot 0 if it's free, else slot 1, where any other takes slot 1 and moves the one there to slot 0 (0xdcc8e-0xdccf4). On e40 and the tutorial the specular light is the pair's second in the file, so both readings agree; on sdm_e10, sdm_e13 and sdm_e40 it's the first, so slot 0 is the key there, not the file's second (`Level::slots` follows the rule, for the lava's v0 and, since #288, the terrain's bump map: 31 levels list the key first). Slot 0's colour (0x41b478) is copied to world+0xba0: BF_LavaMaterial's v0. **Corrected (#271):** the list's order is the level file's reversed (0x14f2a0 puts each directional light at its front), so the lights are met last to first: of two keys or two fills the file's second takes slot 0, and a key takes it whichever the file lists first (#288; `bf/level.rs` `file_slots`); one fill alone, slot 1. The direction is the light matrix's third column as it is (bytes 8, 0x14, 0x20; not normalised, and vertex program 0x19 takes `max(0, n . c16)` against it: 34 of the 161 directional placements are off unit length by over 2%), the colour FUN_0009e9c0's (h_f08eb2f3 x max(0, 1 - type+0x4c x light+0x78)); an empty slot 0 gets direction (0, 1, 1) and black. Vertex program 0x38 (0x3d8bf8, the terrain's base pass by its constants; no code found loading it) lights with slot 1 alone (c18 / c19) and gives slot 0's direction (c16) to the bump map. In a vision mode (its second argument, the view's vision mode not 0, 0x9f443-0x9f45a) a fixed pair in place of either: colour (1, 1, 1) (1.0 at 0x40d5b8) from straight up and straight down (the table at 0x41b3a8, filled at 0xdd10e-0xdd1dc; its c17.w 1 and c18.w 0.5 are the heat figures' sphere map's), the ambient left. The takes' Vengar views are lit at about a third of that white: what scales it isn't traced. | code, live (#35 take08: the sunlit tower dark, the shaded sand bright) | `play_vengar.rs` (`vision_lights`, `vision_terrain`, `VISION_LIGHT`); `bf/level.rs` (`Level::pair`, `Level::slots`, `file_slots`, `LevelLight::strength`); `level_scene.rs` (`spawn_lighting`, `light_buffer`, `terrain_bump`); lamps.wgsl (`indoor_swap`, TERRAIN_BUMP); levels.md § Light objects, § Terrain layers (the bump map) |

FUN_000e5d40 lays the result over the picture with program 0x3a and shader 0x3a (0x3e15e0: t0 as it is), ONE / ONE when the byte 0x3c06e5 is set, z off. **Correction (#289):** this row had "which pixels its stencil test keeps isn't read": there is no stencil test. Before the first pass it takes the device's render target (+0x15b4) and depth-stencil surface (+0x15b8), retypes both as textures (resource type 0x40000) and binds the back buffer as LIN_A8R8G8B8 (format byte 0x12) at stage 0 and the depth-stencil as LIN_R8G8B8A8 (0x41) at stage 1, so t1.a is the D24S8 stencil byte: each pixel's stencil value / 255 is its luminance threshold, and 255 (FUN_000d4180's, the clear's) is never passed. Both stages CLAMP; COLORWRITEENABLE all (+0x4ec 0x01010101), ZENABLE 0, CULLMODE none; ONE / ZERO into h_0796c3b0 (320 x 240, FUN_000e49d0), cleared and scissored when the viewport isn't the whole 640 x 480. Program 0x39's c24-c25: the viewport's size and corner, the target's share of it (+0xc, +0x10: FUN_000e4ca0) and its corner there. Pixel shader 0x1f: r1 = c0 . t0 (blue to alpha); r0 = t1.a + 0.5 - r1.a; r0 = mux(t0, 0) (MSB: 0 where t1.a >= the luminance); r0 x t0.a; final r0 x v0 (white), alpha min(1, r + g + b). Second pass: the 160 x 120 target h_0575b9b9 cleared, ALPHABLENDENABLE ONE / ONE, program 0x56 (c24 = +0xc, +0x10; c25 = their halves, 0x4a5b88 twice: never written, 0), shader 0x20 (r1.a = t0 . t0; final v0 x t0), the first target through its linear header (+0xfc) at stage 0, the 24 quads of FUN_000e47b0 (each x 25 / 255: 2.35 times the kept picture, saturating). Runs in vision mode 0 (0xe8755), 1 (0xe8a67, reached by the mode-1 branch at 0xe8987: FUN_000e6a70, FUN_000d4180 at 0xe899b, then the +0xb44 test at 0xe8a57) and 2 (0xe8917, after the heat figures), whenever +0xb44 (FUN_0009e960, 0xa8c80, 0xa2990, and the glare object's own write at 0xe46a5; FUN_000d9f00 clears it, 0xda1f8). Between keys, FUN_002df650 / FUN_002df730. drive them (#222).

| `FUN_000124f0` | Sets a brain timer (vtable 0x3a4024, whose tick 0x124a0 counts it down by the AI dt; the brain's timer list at brain+0xb0, vtable 0x3a47b0, is ticked by 0x12470 at the top of each AI update, 0x1b0d7): it overwrites what was left. The reactions' cooldowns are such timers: brain+0xd4 KNOCKDOWN, +0xe4 STUN, +0xf4 DIVE, +0x104 DODGE. | `FUN_00059000` | The A* `(start, goal, &list)` over the points' own link lists (+0x30), forward from the start: records in DAT_004a5900 (FUN_00058e10 by point+0x26), a binary heap on f (FUN_001611d0 + FUN_00059920 to push, FUN_00059850 to pop), seeded with the start (g 0, h FUN_00058ef0), stopped when the goal comes off (0x590fe). A neighbour needs +0x28 bit 3 clear and bit 2 set, but passes without bit 2 while the record vector holds only the start (its first neighbours met before any passes: a character whose own point is invalid still gets out), and lies within DAT_004021a8 of the start when that's positive (-1.0 in the binary; GOAL_ATTACK's approach sets it). **g is truncated at each step**: the parent's g + FUN_00058f50 is stored as a float, `cvttss2si` (0x591e1) and `fild` back (0x59201, 0x5920f), so a link under 0.5 m costs nothing. A neighbour neither open nor closed is taken, else only for a strictly lower g (0x5920a); taken: parent, g, h, f = h + g; a closed one opened again; one not open pushed. An open one whose f falls isn't moved in the heap (no sift for it). No node or time cap. The path rebuilt from the goal by the parents, each pushed at the list's front: start ... goal. | code | nav.md; todo/242 leads §1.3; `bf/nav.rs` (`Graph::search`, `push_heap`, `pop_heap`) |
